member of the commission. mr president, ladies and gentlemen, the restructuring plan announced by volkswagen to the belgian company's works council and the press in november has a major social and economic impact within and beyond the company. first of all, i would like to express my very serious concern about the consequences that the implementation of this plan may have for several thousand workers and their families and for the economic and social environment of the company. restructuring is sometimes necessary to ensure the competitiveness and sustainability of firms and, ultimately, employment. i am aware of the need for volkswagen, as for many other european companies in this sector, to undergo restructuring. but it is important to ensure that this process is well anticipated and well managed in order to minimise the economic and social costs. for this reason, the commission adopted a communication on restructuring and employment in march 2005. this communication announced the main elements of a proactive strategy to which you fully subscribed, namely anticipation, partnership and coordination of policies and instruments. the commission has made every effort to use its own instruments since the announcement of the restructuring by volkswagen's management. i met with the members of the belgian government to express our solidarity and to stress the importance of respecting the rules of community law applicable, in particular the directives on information and consultation of workers' representatives. my colleague danuta hübner and i have also made known the availability of the commission services for a flexible use of financial support instruments, in particular the structural funds, to help workers affected by restructuring. the european social fund has demonstrated in the past how targeted and rapid action can help reintegrate workers made redundant as part of company restructuring into the labour market. the commission services were invited to participate in the crisis unit set up by the belgian authorities, which will examine, among other aspects, the practical possibilities for using the appropriations: either those remaining from the current programming expiring at the end of 2006, or those from the new programmes coming into force from 2007 onwards, for which there are more available. in any event, i can tell you that the commission is willing to be proactive and as flexible as possible in the use of the european budget. on 24 november, the commission held a meeting of its task force on restructuring set up in the wake of the march 2005 communication on restructuring and employment. the aim of the meeting was to mobilise all the instruments at its disposal in order to mitigate the impact of the restructuring measures taken in conjunction with the belgian authorities and to incorporate the developments in the automotive sector into a strategy for the future in europe. the automotive sector is vital for europe: it represents 3% of european gdp and employs 12 million people; it makes a significant contribution to the research and development effort; it creates a trade surplus with the rest of the world of about 25 billion euros annually. the automotive sector, like all other sectors of the economy, is facing profound changes. nevertheless, it has been able to adapt to the major changes it is facing. the figures i have just given are a good indication of this. apart from the specific case we are dealing with today, restructuring in the automobile sector is, in general, part of a necessary adaptation process. the commission, however, neither wants nor can substitute itself for the obligations and responsibilities of the company in the restructuring process under way. i have personally written to the president of the volkswagen group to express our concern and to ask him to do everything possible to minimise the economic and social costs of the announced restructuring. i reminded him of the need to ensure that the community directives on information and consultation of workers are respected in all the member states. these community texts provide for prior information and consultation of workers' representatives at the appropriate level on the transfer of production and the redundancies it entails. it is crucial to ensure that all information and all useful consultation is held at the appropriate level, both on economic decisions and on their employment consequences. i would also remind you that in 2003, in response to a commission consultation on this subject, the european social partners jointly developed reference guidelines for managing change and its social consequences. these reference guidelines state that the existence of a quality social dialogue in a climate of trust and a positive attitude towards change are important factors in avoiding or limiting negative social consequences. the implementation of these guidelines is essential to achieve the community's objectives of anticipating and accompanying change. the commission therefore again approached the european social partners in march 2005 to find effective ways of developing and implementing these guidelines across europe. i intend to meet with the representatives of the social partners in the near future to discuss with them how to move forward in this direction in a more rapid and effective manner. to return to the case which gave rise to this debate, it is now important to leave room for negotiation within the group undergoing restructuring. recent signs indicate that the relevant partners, management and workers' representatives, are engaging in a serious discussion on the industrial future of the various sites in europe, as well as on the social measures to be implemented. while maintaining its offer of support, the commission intends to respect the autonomy of the social dialogue which is beginning within the group. within its field of competence, the commission shall take the initiative and make available the means at its disposal. in the present circumstances, i hope nothing but firm commitment from all parties involved in this same direction.

the rapporteur. mr president, madam minister, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, it is clear that we are living a happy moment in this parliament tonight with the debate on the seventh framework programme and the prospect of its implementation. it is a happy moment, especially for the scientific community, because this programme is the third most important programme of the european union and it is a programme which enables the scientific community to give itself a european dimension and excellence. so, not having any discontinuity from one program to another is a key objective. thanks to the joint efforts of the finnish presidency, whose perseverance and quality of work i commend, the commissioner and the commission and parliament departments - we worked in an excellent spirit of collaboration, respecting the objectives that had been set - we have reached the stage where we can launch the seventh framework programme on time. this is a crucial point. the second point is a little less positive: the means are not up to our expectations. there's nothing we can do about it. the commissioner stressed that the financial perspective had delayed the discussion and then failed to match europe's ambitions. i will repeat again and again that the europe of tomorrow is built on research and innovation. we are talking about it, we are discussing it, but the member states are not always willing to bring their declarations into line with the objectives. it is therefore damaging that the framework programme, as proposed by the commission, has been cut by at least 25 to 30%. but we must, as they say in belgium, try to have the most efficient framework programme possible. in this connection, i believe, as mr buzek has said, that the commission's proposal has been broadly accepted and that the improvements or amendments which have been made are all in the right direction. i would like to make two points. the european research council is obviously a major innovation in european policy. it is essential that the scientific community establishes itself at european level and that excellence is recognised by the european scientific community itself. this will give europe much greater visibility. that is why we, and mrs niebler amongst others, have been very attentive to the way in which this european research council would operate. another point which i think is essential is joint technological initiatives. this process, which is an extension of the technology platforms, is essential to ensure the competitiveness of european industry. it will therefore be very important that the council, the commission and parliament can implement joint technological initiatives in certain areas quickly. it will obviously not be possible to do this everywhere, but i believe that the tool is indispensable. commissioner, you are a greater diplomat than i am and i would like to congratulate you on getting the council to agree to extend stem cell research, including surplus embryonic stem cells. this is a balanced agreement. it is a continuation of what we were able to do with the sixth framework programme, but it is well framed ethically. the statement you have just made reassures our colleagues that we are not destroying embryos but are preserving all the prospects that stem cells offer for the scientific and medical knowledge of tomorrow, in particular the surplus embryonic stem cells. i therefore commend you on reaching this agreement. i would also like to point out to the house that tomorrow we will also be voting on the specific programmes and that there are amendments. i know that the procedure is different for specific programmes, but this is an opinion. that said, there should be no votes on specific programmes which are not in line with what was voted for the framework programme. mr buzek rightly said that parliament supported the compromise proposals. there are also legitimate amendments from members of parliament. the majorities differ according to whether the specific programmes or the framework programme are concerned. for the sake of overall coherence of the system, there should be no gap between specific programmes and framework programmes. i'm coming to the rules of engagement. i shall be very brief because you have stressed, commissioner, the new points in relation to the proposal. but there is one essential thing that i believe we share. the aim is simplification and we have tried to develop in this direction. unfortunately, however, we must remain humble and modest: we are constantly told that we must simplify, but those who have had a great deal of experience with framework programmes have always found that, unfortunately, it is not yet perfect. i'm not sure what we've done will be perfect. it will also depend on your vigilance and not just your vigilance, but also our vigilance. we're here to help you undo the constraints that come from others to complicate the process. for example, we wanted to ensure the participation of small and mediumsized enterprises. but it's not just the fact that we're going to give them 75 percent, it's the fact, for example, that in the guarantee fund, we're not going to ask them to do financial audits every three months to see if they're able to participate. so these are the practical consequences, but they depend on the implementation because sometimes there are people who are overzealous. it's pretty normal, but sometimes it's too much. so you'll have to see to it that it's executed properly. i am also thinking of reducing indirect costs by 60%. it is true that this figure can be considered a bit arbitrary, and that indirect costs must be calculated as closely as possible, which is why a distinction is made between the first three years and the others, but such a figure was essential to have universities and smes in all countries, because accounting systems are very different. i would like to conclude by saying: thank you very much for the work done, finnish presidency, which has been excellent in this area. i thank you again and i think that tomorrow many of us will be able to vote without any problems on your proposals and our amended proposals.

in writing. aids is back! belgium reached a record high with 1 072 new infections in 2005. a 52-percent increase in eight years! people die less from the disease, at least in belgium. but aids has become commonplace. the condom is like a diet you have to follow all year. sometimes you just want to crack. so we must strengthen the means of prevention. and in places strongly influenced by religion, remind them that calls for abstinence are out of place! that's why i co-signed a draft resolution on aids. i wanted to draw attention to the children suffering from aids. fifteen million children are orphaned by the disease, 12 million of them in africa alone! according to professor montagnier, the discoverer of the virus, "the wave of infection will not produce its full effects for another ten years. the demographic impact will be significant, as the disease will wipe out an entire population of young adults". this friday is international aids day. we should not miss this opportunity to remind people of the growing burden of aids. while the death rate from other infectious diseases should be reduced, this one, thankfully, is!
mr president, on 20 september 2003, that is to say before we began our work here, mr chirac, mr blair and mr schroeder wrote to the european commission asking it not to undermine the competitiveness of the chemical industry. the intervention, and of course the pressure, at this level was unprecedented in our history of codecision, and i must say that at that time the message was clear: reach would be far, far from the ambition that was set out, particularly in the white paper, as early as 2001. of course, we cannot afford to put this sector, which is one of the most competitive and dynamic in our industry, at a disadvantage. we have been listening to him, each of us, constantly, to his concerns. reach will be feasible. the evidence is the simplified registration procedure for substances produced in quantities of one to ten tonnes, the strengthening of the future helsinki agency, the affirmation - as has been said many times - of the osor principle (one substance - one registration), in order to facilitate the exchange of information between companies. it is also important to limit the costs of data entry: this is essential for smes. there is also this possibility of opt-out, of exemption, duly justified by the applicant. these are considerable advances, and i would like to be able to celebrate them today if we had not given in to the substitution principle. what remains is not even a dilution, i would rather say it is a deception, a gradual, case-by-case substitution, including carcinogens, mutagens and reproductive toxicants, if adequate control is exercised - and i quote the text here. reach is also, and above all, a blank cheque for endocrine disruptors - plasticisers, insecticides, flame retardants, all of which are among the most dangerous chemicals for human health - a blank cheque signed by our parliament, while the finnish presidency agreed here to impose substitution. i hallucinate when i think about it! europe has turned its back on europeans. explain to them today that a substance that is harmful to health is not withdrawn from the market, especially if there is a safer alternative! i am ashamed too of the end of the non-receipt, and i use a euphemism, of the european bodies opposed to the two million doctors, the bodies and the heads of the scientific world who constantly warn us about this silent pandemic, created, among other things, by chemical pollution. i read the same lancet article as mrs. mcavan and mr. schlyter! so, on wednesday, 13 december, our meeting with the people's europe will be too little, too late, and all those who, like me, here, are betting on combining health and sustainable employment instead of continuing to oppose them in a totally sterile and outdated way, will have today only a minute or two to express their disappointment.

if the commission has any plans on this, i should like to hear them later. we have weighed everything and we will approve the package, mr president, because we know that in 11 years' time we will have radically renewed and made transparent our chemicals policy. europeans will live with fewer health risks and we also expect that the competitiveness of industry will not be weakened but rather strengthened. of course, the companies are paying a price, and not a small price. but the mainstream of society is demanding more health information and more health protection, and that demand is not going to go down. a reach tested label may eventually become a commercial advantage rather than an economic disadvantage. and our legislation, as has been said here before, can become a global standard. we must at least make sure that this is done, including by means of good information campaigns for consumers.
mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to congratulate the rapporteur and i am sure he will be commended for the work he has done, but i regret that the commission has not followed parliament's proposal for a decision correcting the 2002 directive on the circulation of compound feedingstuffs. the obligation to provide the composition of mixtures to authorities in the event of a disaster alone, while a step forward, is not enough and will have to be addressed when the commission reviews the legislation in 2007. it is essential that the origin of the materials, whether vegetable or mineral, be known to the farmer using the food. so a mineral or vegetable protein does not have the same virtues depending on its origin and it is because of the absence of this information that we have found in the food the waste oils that led to the all too famous bse crisis, for example. the composition of the mixtures cannot be concealed under the pretext of intellectual property protection of the formulae. in the course of his work, a farmer who composes the diet of his livestock with part of his own production must be able to supplement it with a mixture which will enable him to balance it. it is therefore essential that he knows the composition of the mixture he is buying, in order to maintain his ration balanced. the farmer must remain in control of his own house and the monopoly of the manufacturers cannot prevent him from doing his job, especially since in the event of a disaster he will be blamed for the misuse of foodstuffs of which he knows nothing. in summary, despite the corrections made to the commission's proposals in this report, the burden on food manufacturers and the agri-food industry at the expense of the farmer is still too great.
the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council and parliament, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. secondly, i would point out that we have succeeded in creating a separate consumer programme, that is to say, a programme with its own budget and which can develop its own dynamic, and i think that consumers will appreciate that too. finally, we have three important new areas of concern: firstly, we have asked for more attention to be paid to new member states with a less long tradition in consumer protection. secondly, we have been able to pay particular attention to older consumers, because demographic change must also be reflected in consumer law. thirdly, in this action programme we have also been able to pay particular attention - and i think this is particularly important - to the vulnerable consumer, who cannot be so well covered in consumer law, where we have to work with the notion of the average consumer. i think that is important, mr president, we did not have a debate, but i thought i should give this information and emphasize these important points; i can only make a warm appeal to vote in favour of the common position, because it fully reflects our agreement in parliament and between the institutions.
in writing. water is the basis of all life and in that sense vital. the european environment agency says the situation is serious: 87% of groundwater in agricultural areas does not meet eu standards for the presence of nitrates and most member states report the risk of pollution by pesticides. a strict new groundwater directive is therefore of the utmost importance. the text adopted by the conciliation committee deserves our support. the ep succeeded in making the notion of 'protection against deterioration' legally binding. it was also ensured that the measures to achieve the quality standard for nitrates should be aligned with the water framework directive. member states and regions are also given more stringent obligations to prevent and reduce the introduction of dangerous substances into groundwater. they now have the obligation to take all the necessary measures to achieve this, not just to 'seek it'. the result of the first reading was a major ecological disappointment. and the council's common position was also a complete disaster. the second reading of the ep and the subsequent conciliation procedure put things right. we can be satisfied with the result. i therefore approved the agreement reached.

vice-president of the commission. mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to thank you for your report. i would like to begin by thanking the rapporteur, mr groote, for his tireless work. it has made it possible to reach agreement with the council at first reading. i would also like to thank the shadow rapporteurs and, of course, the finnish presidency for the efforts which have helped us to bring this difficult chapter to a close now. the three institutions have worked together constructively. we now have a proposal on the table which sets strict emission limits for the next 10 years, while creating incentives and clarity for technological innovation. the proposals are also based on a thorough impact assessment, which confirmed the technical and financial feasibility of the proposals, as a result of a concerted effort. the proposed regulation is a major step towards improving the environmental performance of motor vehicles. poor air quality throughout the european union is a matter of great concern and has serious health implications. therefore, this regulation deals first with particulate matter emissions and with the emission of nitrogen oxides. so it's about improving air quality. the commission is in favour of the agreement at first reading for four reasons. firstly, the compromise reached takes into account the costs and benefits of setting emission limit values. for petrol-engined vehicles, more stringent limit values have been set than for diesel vehicles, as lower emissions are easier and cheaper to achieve for petrol-engined vehicles than for diesel engines. this is important if we are to persuade consumers to switch from their old, more polluting vehicles to more environmentally friendly but more expensive ones. secondly, the commission welcomes the timetable for the introduction of euro 5 and euro 6. the five-year gap between the introduction of the two standards is important to allow the engines to be developed accordingly to meet the next and more stringent emission level. thirdly, i am pleased that an agreement has been reached on access to repair and maintenance information. the oasis standard will be maintained for the time being and can then be adapted rapidly to technical progress. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the environmental impact of new vehicles is not only limited to their new state, but also extends over their entire lifetime. however, this cannot be guaranteed without access to repair and maintenance information. fourthly, i think it is important that a consensus has been reached on the question of financial incentives, a package of two emission levels, as in euro 3 and euro 4 and now again in euro 5 and euro 6. this has environmental and technological benefits if both stages are actually implemented as stages. the same must apply to tax incentives. the commission has therefore decided to accept all the amendments proposed by your rapporteur, mr groote.
draft report by the committee on economic and monetary affairs and industrial policy on the proposal from the commission to the council (com (90) 0143 - c4-0414/91) for a directive on the approximation of the laws of the member states relating to the labelling of foodstuffs. mr president, this directive is obviously - and i thank the rapporteur for that - a major step forward in the field of public health. the compulsory installation of soot filters on diesel cars in particular is reducing fine dust emissions by 80%, which is a figure that should be welcomed by everyone. at present, life expectancy in the european union is on average 8 months lower due to the presence of ultra-harmful fine particulate matter, soot particles in the air, and any measure to address this situation is of course positive. it is now up to the member states to introduce measures, fiscal and other incentives to get older, polluting diesels off the road as quickly as possible. so, unfortunately, ladies and gentlemen, there are also some disadvantages. it is truly regrettable that the 4x4s have been given an exception through a back gate and that the manufacturers of these so-called suvs are given more time to adapt. i assume, commissioner, that you yourself do not drive an suv for the simple reason that suvs, you know, are the biggest distributors of fine dust; they emit 1/3 more fine dust than normal diesel cars and fine dust causes cancer, you know that. i therefore call on the manufacturers to take their responsibility. the brand of off-road vehicle that comes up with the fastest environmentally friendly off-road vehicle, in my eyes and in the eyes of all of us, i think, is miles ahead of the competition. finally, ladies and gentlemen, public opinion can and must play its part. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's energy policy is not undermined by the fact that the community's energy policy is not being implemented.
president. - madam president, ladies and gentlemen, the jokes about twins have become tiresome recently, but i was just about to say that fortunately you are not evil twins yet, because that would be a great pity. ladies and gentlemen, since may/june 2005, enlargement has been under attack. the main reason for the rejection in france and the netherlands was enlargement. in this negative atmosphere, the concept of absorptive capacity was suddenly introduced. a terribly ugly word and actually an excuse for many to hold back further enlargement and even to call into question, if not revoke, the promises made. my group has never been in favour of this. my group is very much attached to the following elements. first of all: the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council and parliament on the subject. secondly: the fulfilment of all commitments made: the european union has made a number of promises to the countries of the western balkans, even though some of them are still in the process of being formed, and also to turkey. in this context, i would like to congratulate commissioner rehn, his colleagues in the commission and the council on the decisions taken with regard to turkey. i think that's a balanced balanced approach. there are no closing doors, but there is a clarification of the issue and we fully support you in that decision. finally, my group is also very keen - president. - mr president, i am not going to go into the details of the enlargement negotiations, but i would like to ask you to give me a brief explanation of the reasons why you have not done so. turkey is given a deadline to fulfil its obligations in the customs union. the turkish government refuses to do so and what is the so-called sanction of the european union. the difficult chapters are not being opened for the time being. the deadline has passed and there will be no new deadline. the commission and the council keep on saying that they do not want a train wreck with turkey, that the turks have understood that they can make conditions and not us. the belgian government has, in fact, devised a new doctrine for this purpose, which is that, with turkey, and i quote: "and that a different approach to that must be taken". in other words, a non-european candidate country like turkey should not be so strict with the copenhagen criteria. the european union has no answer to turkey's bazaar negotiating style. commissioner rehn has just spoken about the credibility of the european union vis-à-vis the applicant countries. he forgets one small detail, namely the credibility of the eu in the eyes of european public opinion. the people have always been told that if turkey failed to make a manifest effort, the negotiations would be stopped. the opposite is happening now, and that is the problem of credibility facing the european union.
in writing. reach is a very important step for eu chemicals policy. the reversal of the principle of responsibility, which passes from the public authorities to industry and producers, is a phenomenal revolution for the whole sector. nor can we underestimate the tremendous knowledge gains that will result from the registration, evaluation and authorisation process. this knowledge gain will have consequences for the whole industry and for our possibilities to preserve man and nature. the only major regret is that the substitution principle for chemicals of "high concern" will not be as binding as might have been hoped. i hope that in the near future this principle will be taken up and accepted by the two european co-legislators and that it will not have to wait twenty years for this, as with this directive. to reject the whole of reach legislation because of this regret would have been to throw the baby out with the bath water and it would certainly not have led to better legislation in the short or medium term. i call on the future agency to remain pragmatic in its attitude to certain well-known substances, the use of which is in principle not a problem, such as lime.
in writing. i cannot possibly be happy about the reach proposal which has now been chased through parliament. the commission, the council and parliament have been led astray by economic blindness and complacency. for the first time in a long time, the union had the opportunity to create a set of rules which all citizens would have seen fit to use: the commission's proposal is a very complex one, but it is concrete and always with a view to the health of all its inhabitants. neither industry nor the public is satisfied with the half-hearted approach to harmful chemicals. some conclude that it is a 'good compromise'. it's not that. it is as if saying that halving the thickness of the berlin wall would have been a good solution. you don't play with people's health. there is no middle ground. even a little healthier is still sick. in flanders, the situation has reached the point where there is no place where no harmful substances are present. often we know they're unhealthy, but we don't know exactly why. a good reach regulation would have solved that problem and would have made flanders healthy in the medium term, for example. what's voted now doesn't do that. half as healthy is not an option.
in writing. first of all, i would like to highlight the excellent work of the rapporteur, mr guido sacconi. i have decided to vote in favour of the compromise amendment between the council and parliament, first and foremost because it will allow for better control of certain dangerous products and at the same time strengthen the competitiveness of the chemical industry. although it is true that this compromise is not perfect, i think that if it had not been adopted, the situation would have been more complicated. the compromise provides for the compulsory substitution of the most dangerous substances where alternatives exist, the recognition of a 'duty of vigilance' on the part of producers and the promotion of alternative methods to animal testing. this version of the text is certainly different from the text submitted at first reading, but if the text had been rejected, we would have had to go through the conciliation stage which would have led to other less favourable conclusions. that is why i thought it wiser to vote for this version of the text, which is not perfect, but which nevertheless allows for progress and greater control of the chemicals we encounter in everyday life.
the report, mr president, is important to me because it considers all the possibilities and alternatives for minimising the number of animal experiments. the suffering of laboratory animals, and of mammals in particular, is a matter of concern to many european citizens. the use of experimental animals should be avoided wherever possible. the european centre for the validation of alternative methods seems to have an important role to play. the institution will have to provide sufficient validated alternatives to end long-term animal testing with more resources. we also call for transparency and clarity on the number of animals and types of tests carried out by research laboratories.
it is difficult, mr president, to be enthusiastic about a text and a vote which is very complicated and which we will only be able to judge properly when we have read it over again in our own time. i have the impression that we have managed to strike a balance between respecting the competitive aspirations of our chemical industry on the one hand and the care and attention so necessary for our public health on the other. in any case, we must note with satisfaction that in recent years it has become clear that our chemical industry is clearly aware of its responsibilities and has taken on the burden of proof and accepted the principle of the duty of care. one more thing: the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's energy policy is not distorted by the fact that the community's energy policy is not based on the principle of subsidiarity.
in writing. i voted for the excellent report by mrs hieronymi on the revision of the european television without frontiers directive to incorporate the breakthrough technological innovations. i welcome the fact that the directive maintains the course on production quotas for european works and the protection of minors against certain abuses. promoting cultural diversity and european values of tolerance also means curbing incitement to hatred, regardless of the channel of transmission - satellite television or the internet. that is why i supported the amendments calling for the extension of 'filtering' to non-linear services: this is the best way to prevent programmes which in the future promote terrorism from being broadcast on european territory! finally, i applaud the resolutely modern vote of the ep today, which recognises the considerable contribution of advertising in the creation of quality independent works and entertainment television that meets the expectations of citizens. i would also point out that the commercial broadcasters are the ones who are setting the example: with more than 30% of their programme orders from independent persons, they not only largely meet the 10% quota introduced by the directive, but above all they spend twice as much as their public competitors.
in writing. the development of the audiovisual sector is enormous. in this sense, it is a good thing that the 'television without frontiers' directive is being revised. the distinction between linear (traditional tv, internet, mobile telephony) and non-linear (on demand) services calls for the formulation of basic rules for the protection of young people against incitement to racial hatred or to prevent subtle advertising. the text adopted has good and bad elements. the good ones include a scheme for the transmission of short segments of football matches or other events. the proposal for a minimum time limit for interrupting programmes by advertising (45 minutes) is also to be defended. the european greens regret that the proposal to limit advertising breaks to a maximum of three per hour did not succeed. and there's a cloud of uncertainty about product placement. we leave it to the member states to decide. the line between product placement and production aid is very thin, which will create doubt and controversy. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of the term 'common carrier' to cover all transport services. it is also a pity that the reference to pluralism and the prevention of media concentration is only in the recitals and not in the articles.
in writing. i have decided to vote in favour of the amended hieronymi report because it represents a great deal of progress in the field of television. i welcome, among other things, the adoption of amendment no 227 banning product placement in television news, political information programmes, children's programmes, documentaries and advice programmes, and allowing it under strict conditions in cinematographic works, films, television series and sports programmes. i have two major criticisms of this report, however. i regret that parliament voted unanimously to suspend the transmission of films made for television, motion pictures, children's programmes and television newspapers by cutting advertising every 30 minutes instead of the 45 minutes voted for in the committee on culture. i also regret the vote on amendment no 221 concerning derogations from the country of origin principle which allows member states to adopt more detailed or stricter rules; the amendment is a danger to cultural diversity, a principle which the european parliament must safeguard.
in writing. europe will help victims of business relocations. from 1 january, a globalisation adjustment fund will provide up to €500 million each year to workers affected by international restructuring. they will be able to count on the union to facilitate, or rather make less difficult, their conversion. individual training, micro-credit, mobility aids, it is clearly a matter of helping employees and not companies. the question obviously arises in all our minds: can the workers of vw forest count on this european solidarity? the criteria for access to the fund are strict: redundancies must affect at least 1000 people, including subcontractors, and have a serious impact on the local economy. but above all, the belgian authorities must be able to prove that the restructuring is the result of 'structural changes in world trade trends', and that is not an easy task! the union is faced here with a clear case of social competition within its territory, and the commission will have to be flexible in its examination of the criteria. it is a question of the image of europe and our concept of solidarity at union level!
in writing. mass redundancies due to closures, heavy restructuring in sectors sensitive to globalisation, less large-scale collective redundancies due to structural changes in world trade patterns ... they all have a big impact on the local labour market. the creation of a european fund for adaptation to globalisation is therefore a good thing. unlike the other structural funds, the globalisation adjustment fund enables member states to respond quickly and directly to acute socioeconomic emergencies, enabling affected workers to reintegrate into the labour market after dismissal. the fact that member states are co-financing 50% of this and that support from this fund is only complementary to and not replacing these actions is positive. i am also glad that the council and the commission support the idea of microcredit as an active labour market measure. the positive side is that older workers are not obliged to work longer for less pay, but are given financial incentives to remain in the labour market. finally, the fact that the fund is structured in such a way that the interventions cannot be intercepted by 'other actors' but benefit the workers directly can only serve as an example for other structural funds.
the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. it is clear that the european union is not prepared to accept the brutal negotiating methods of turkey, which my colleague has sarcastically, but very rightly, described as 'bazaar negotiations'. the way in which the european negotiators let their own ultimatums lapse again and again with impunity is outrageous and gives turkey a totally wrong image. the image of a european union which is prepared to do anything to ensure turkish accession, and this is absolutely against the democratic will of the majority of european citizens. we may have to conclude that the european union is as undemocratic as the applicant country turkey.
the turkish government has indicated that it will not give in to what it describes as pressure from the cypriots to force ankara to take so-called unilateral measures to resolve the problems between turkey and nicosia. it is said that the european union, to which turkey is so eager to belong, has yielded to the pressure from cyprus. the turkish government has now announced that it will not take any so-called unilateral measures, in other words, that it will not comply with the agreements contained in the ankara protocol on cyprus. i hope, ladies and gentlemen, that the european council tomorrow and the day after tomorrow will take the opportunity to clarify the situation with regard to turkey, because what is at stake here are not unilateral measures but a situation which is crystal clear and which can be summed up in a few sentences. it is not possible for a candidate country which has been occupying the territory of another member state of the union for more than 30 years to join the union and then think that it can pass the blame on to the victims. that's the gist of the matter. the fact that erdogan refuses to open his ports and airports to cypriot ships is only the logical consequence of turkish imperialism towards cyprus. the negotiations must not be suspended, but must be stopped.
president, madam president-in-office, mr president of the commission, i cannot imagine that the council would not say anything about the restructuring of volkswagen vorst. the commission has already taken a number of decisions on the basis of the information available to it. i think that europe can help not only through the structural funds or a globalisation fund, but i think that the commission must also look after the restructuring. were the unions consulted in time? i think that is certainly not the case at volkswagen! is the restructuring based on economic motives or were there other national motives? volkswagen vorst is the second most productive site. it is true that the automobile sector is overproduced, but i think that it is precisely at such a time that a real industrial policy is needed. cars 21 is a first step, but it seems to me that it is still far from being enough.
president of the council. i would simply like to point out, mr president, that the free movement of persons is one of the fundamental issues of the european union. the mobility of services is also well developed and if there are irregularities in the member states, it is the duty and the duty of the member states to monitor and deal with them. it is clear that the wide disparities in the legislation of the member states lead to very different practices across europe.
i have to say that i am not in favour of this. the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect. it was a tough job. we started working on it two and a half years ago. the electoral period was not the most favourable for reaching an agreement at first reading, but we have brought the dossier to a good end in my view. i would like to thank my colleagues in advance because we had a very open debate in the committee and also found that it was not so much about party differences as it was about traditions. i would like to thank in particular those colleagues who have given their support to the project, far beyond the sensitivities in their own country. i would also like to thank the council, because the finnish presidency, the austrian presidency and, last but not least, the luxembourg presidency, which laid the foundations for the compromise, have all contributed to the fact that we can reach a good conclusion tomorrow. mr commissioner, i also want to thank you for your very efficient support of this project. i would like to emphasize in particular the expertise of the members of your administration in dealing with this very complex dossier, which has led us to some, i believe, quite interesting solutions. the challenges of this directive can, in my view, be summed up in the following points: the first thing we wanted to do was not to introduce an additional driving licence, the 111 or the 130 but to introduce a single european driving licence in due course. but this was more of a symbol, because we also wanted to ensure that the exchange of driving licences would record driving licences in circulation and we have the corresponding documents, so that you know exactly how many driving licences are in circulation in different countries and who owns them, so that the exchange of information can take place. finally, road safety was also a concern, and this was particularly the case in the motorcycle sector. we have achieved that. the directive also took account of a good balance between economic efficiency and safety, particularly in the caravan sector. these common challenges have led to a common position. the countries will introduce the new model of driving licence in six years' time for new applications. this affects, albeit progressively, 300 million citizens. but it is more than just a symbol for these citizens. it is precisely this added value that has convinced us all. there's an improvement in mobility. with a single driving licence recognised everywhere the problems associated with the recognition of acquired rights in the respective countries will disappear not only for tourists but also for professional drivers. that will be ensured once and for all by this proposal today. legal certainty is one of our main concerns, and we have created it with this project. since we are dealing with a compromise, we must talk about deadlines. i would have been very happy, of course, if the whole process, both the first exchange and the regular exchange, had been completed within ten years. the commission has also proposed that the council should adopt a decision on the subject. i'm going to start with a little philosophical principle here: better late than never. but i hope that this will happen a little earlier than the deadline set by the directive. one of the most important aspects in my view is still safety. we have made two important points in this area. the first is to stop so-called driver's licence tourism. in the event of withdrawal, restriction or suspension, driving tourism can be stopped, based on the data we have in the various countries and if the countries show good will. we believe that anyone who is dangerous on the road in one country should be banned from driving in the other 26 countries. the basis is laid, and we are waiting for a clear signal from the member states. a second important point in safety was the motorcycle area. the progressive access to heavy motorcycles, which may be expected in time in other areas, is a principle based on statistics, which are unfortunately not yet as positive in the motorcycle sector as regards accidents, but on the other hand it also represents a good combination of practice and theory. the theory at the beginning and then the practice should make access easy. in the caravan sector, a good balance between economy and safety has been found. i am glad that the commission has accepted our proposal. we have not only called for the additional b+e licence for motorhomes and caravans, but also for a small test for those over a certain weight. in conclusion, i would say that this directive, thanks to the good cooperation of the three institutions, but also of the members of this parliament, represents an added value for the citizens: more legal certainty, more road safety, more mobility, less driving tourism and also a plus for business and tourism. in this sense, i think that overall, with the report we have set a good example of what is possible in the committee on transport in terms of pragmatism.
the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's environmental policy is not distorted. madam president, i am speaking in my capacity as rapporteur for the opinion of the committee on the environment on mr seppänen's report. the committee on the environment broadly welcomes the report drawn up by the committee on industry. i shall use the minute i have to highlight the following particular points: first, the support which the community will have to give to the development, under the aegis of the iaea, of a code of conduct for the international nuclear incident alert system; secondly, the importance of the polluter pays principle in holding third countries and operators to account. thirdly, financing the rehabilitation of former nuclear sites, on the express condition that a high degree of safety is guaranteed at low cost and within a reasonable time. it is regrettable, however, that our amendments on the amendment of the legal basis, the taking into account of the independent audits of the european court of auditors, or the budgetary rigour in consultation, to name but a few, were not accepted. i am also personally pleased that the committee on industry has taken up my amendment amending recital 13, which recalls that the member states are sovereign when it comes to making choices in the nuclear field. what applies to austria does not necessarily apply to finland or belgium.
in writing. by 2032, our grandchildren will all have the same driver's license! a single licence for all europeans, in the form of a credit card, a driving licence made in the usa: that is a guarantee of increased safety and a concrete sign of european identification. i regret that it took 26 years to achieve harmonization; however, the very first licences will be issued in 2012. it's about time! there are now 110 different driving licences in the european union! it creates confusion for the less honest and makes the road more dangerous than it needs to be. harmonizing national legislation means fighting 'driving licence tourism', i.e. the possibility for european citizens who have had their driving licence withdrawn in their country as a result of a serious offence to obtain another licence elsewhere in the union. the rules for the training of examiners will also be harmonised. this is a great way of ensuring the quality of education throughout europe.
mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, we all know how important the efficiency of markets is to the world economy. the market is liquidity and the ability of financial instruments to move quickly and in optimum safety. the legal framework governing such traffic is an essential parameter in terms of safety. globalisation has led to a chain of actors covering an increasing number of countries, raising the legitimate question of how to determine which law is applicable. the hague convention of 13 december 2002 was intended to answer this question. if the convention were to be ratified, far from solving this problem, it would increase systemic risks and weaken european actors. in view of the supposed impossibility of defining an objective place for the location of a securities account and, therefore, of determining the applicable law, the convention gives priority to the free choice of the parties to decide which law will apply to the contracts. this legal principle is in conflict with the community prima principle of the location of the securities account, adopted in three directives, the purpose directive (1998), the collateral directive (2002) and, finally, the credit institutions directive (2001). let us first consider the method. it is not right that parliament has not been consulted on the negotiations in the hague, in particular on the abandonment of the principle of the location of the securities account. the negotiations in the hague conference on private international law must be subject to democratic control. as far as the substance is concerned, the adoption of this convention would pose a significant risk to europe, from investors to issuers. it would have a negative impact on the fight against money laundering as it would facilitate the possible circumvention of reporting obligations and anti-money laundering mechanisms. it would have implications for delivery settlement systems, prudential risk and substantive law, in particular the objective of developing a european substantive law. it should be added that free choice of contract would in many cases be the most influential law in this sector, i.e. us law, which would reduce the competitiveness of european companies. i would like to point out four things: firstly, the location of the title account is possible and works very well in europe; secondly, the prerogatives of the european parliament must be respected. thirdly, it is essential to defend the general european interest, in particular the rights of european investors and the competitiveness of european players on financial markets. and fourthly, we must give priority to a framework to combat money laundering. the commission should therefore withdraw its proposal for ratification of the 36th hague convention.
author. mr president, ladies and gentlemen, a little over a year ago i spoke here about the situation in burma. the words repression, military junta in power, human rights violated, already smeared my speech. unfortunately, this situation has not changed: burma still makes no effort to make the exercise of power more democratic. several offices of the international red cross were closed by the spdc, making the humanitarian work of the organization impossible. the government prevents ngos from working properly, which causes many of them to leave. the military aid provided by india also arouses a great misunderstanding on our part: does not this country proclaim itself the largest democracy in the world? this is a total nonsense, which we condemn. india cannot ignore that this military aid will be used for the purpose of suppressing ethnic groups and political opponents! i think it is necessary for the un security council to take firm action against burma, condemning this illegitimate regime and calling for the restoration of democracy. i would also like to stress the feeling of confidence i have in the positive influence that the other asean countries can have: by their concern for greater integration, they play a role, which in my view is not negligible, in the field of human rights. may china and india play a similar role in the future, so that the burmese regime can move towards democracy!
mr president, like my good friend mr farage, i fear that i too will break the consensus on this very point. failed european summits are becoming part of the tradition. the failure of the presidency is always euphemised in fine terms in order to avoid the presidency being too much in the way. but this summit on the enlargement strategy, which is suddenly being put on a slower pace, has again been for nothing. the accession negotiations with turkey are being frozen for the time being, as they are called, while ankara's attitude towards cyprus, but also in other areas such as freedom of the press and the armenian question, should not lead to a freeze but to a definitive halt to the negotiations. i am surprised and annoyed that some spokesmen for official europe think they have to interfere with the freedom of the press and the internal politics of another member state. what a paradox! we must and will eventually welcome asian turkey, as we have been told for years, but we learned last week that the small prosperous country of flanders, located in the centre of europe, must not become an independent member of the eu. the commission is also aware of the fact that the european parliament has been involved in the negotiations on the accession of the new member states to the european union, and that the council has been involved in the negotiations on the accession of the new member states to the european union. europe cannot tolerate this, it seems. the flemings know what they are dealing with with these heads of state and government. mr juncker and his colleagues decree, without tolerating contradiction, that the belgian monarchy must continue to exist. nobody else has a finger in the pie. it is not a sign of great respect for freedom of expression, let alone the right of peoples to self-determination, when foreign heads of state think they can interfere with the freedom of the press in a member state and ignore the wishes of the flemings. but, ladies and gentlemen, the flemish people's will is very democratic and legitimate. flanders wants to be free of the corrupt ps dictatorship in wallonia and also wants to be free of the crooked royal children of the house of saxe-coburg. we want to make use of the right to self-determination, as the czechs and slovaks do. it is not for the european heads of state to dictate to flanders how it will take control of its future.
